Automatically saves dated reports and tracks evidence-backed improvements across runs.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# AIOS Audit\\r\\n\\r\\nCheck whether this AIOS can find the right information and do useful work reliably, and identify changes that would make the user\'s work easier. Score **Context, Connections, Capabilities, and Cadence out of 25 each**, using [rubric.md](rubric.md). This is rubric **v2**. Label the number **verified operational reliability**, not overall usefulness or percentage of work the AIOS can do. A folder, installed skill, API key, or confident claim is not proof of a working system.\\r\\n\\r\\nRun locally in the current project. Do not launch subagents unless the user requests them. Audit first; `/link` repairs a route, and `/level-up` helps close one meaningful gap. Never inflate or deliberately depress a score to encourage another run. Improvement comes from better evidence and behavior.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Every audit automatically saves its report in the audited project\'s `audits/` folder.** Read [history.md](history.md) for prior-report selection, finding transitions, comparison rules, and safe persistence. Saving the local audit record is part of this skill, not a separate approval step. An explicit user request not to save overrides this default. The inspected system remains unchanged.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Establish scope and evidence\\r\\n\\r\\n- Get the current date and project root. Read applicable `AGENTS.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and local overrides; follow the runtime\'s instruction hierarchy. Read both manuals when both exist. Check for conflicting routing and promised synchronization, without assuming distinct runtime instructions must be identical.\\r\\n- Load the relevant prior AIOS report and carried finding ledger using [history.md](history.md), before selecting probes. Recheck important prior findings alongside current priorities; do not assume earlier results remain true. Identify the comparison baseline and scope changes before scoring.\\r\\n- Use the manual\'s routing rules first. Read its project and knowledge indexes before listing their immediate child folders. Inspect only relevant scoped manuals. Skip dependencies, caches, generated output, and archives unless a route points there or an archived/current ambiguity needs checking.\\r\\n- Identify the user\'s role, main objective, current priorities, and up to three important workflows from actual context. Unfilled template placeholders do not count. Do not require business revenue data from someone whose work has no revenue function.\\r\\n- Inspect the supported skill/agent registries, connection references, and actual scheduler configuration/run evidence. Accept scripts, MCPs, exports, local data, and equivalent mechanisms equally. Count inventories for orientation only.\\r\\n- Record each finding as **verified**, **documented but unverified**, **missing**, **stale**, or **conflicting**, with a file/line, source URL, or dated run reference. File modification times and last-checked labels alone do not prove a successful refresh or execution.\\r\\n- Start with targeted reads. If a time budget or local page limit prevents verification, mark it unverified and state coverage. Never award full points because there was no time to check. Do not claim an exhaustive audit from a sample.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Test routing and context retrieval\\r\\n\\r\\nMake a compact map: **user need → manual/index → specific source → freshness rule**. Include applicable business context, people, priorities/commitments, original records, project deliverables, specialist knowledge bases, media/assets, and external systems. A generic folder or provider name is insufficient when it leaves the relevant source unclear.\\r\\n\\r\\nCompare the maps with immediate folders to find important unindexed projects, outdated counts, broken paths, ambiguous aliases, duplicate sources of truth, and retired material presented as active. Distinguish confirmed gaps from folders whose relevance is unknown. Check whether supplied routes work from the current checkout, including references inside skills and generated mirrors where that runtime uses them.\\r\\n\\r\\nFollow the declared routes for these five questions, adapted to the user\'s real work:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. What does this person/business do, for whom, and what matters now?\\r\\n2. Where is the authoritative current priority, commitment, or status, and how would I verify it?\\r\\n3. Where is the latest deliverable and next step for an important active project?\\r\\n4. Where is a previous decision, lesson, or subject-specific knowledge item and its supporting source?\\r\\n5. Where is one commonly needed external record, asset, or original document, and how is it accessed?\\r\\n\\r\\nSelect relevant examples before searching for answers. If a category does not apply, record why and substitute a different real retrieval need, keeping five probes. For each, show the question, route attempted, source, result, and whether it was found directly, only by fallback search, or not found. A broad search can recover an answer, but it does not prove the declared route works. Mark external access unverified if not checked; a documented access path earns only documentation credit. Score only the evidence actually observed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Check freshness and authority explicitly:**\\r\\n\\r\\n- An optional `_hot.md`, briefing, or cache earns no points just for existing. Do not recommend creating one by default. Check whether it duplicates canonical pages, has a real refresh mechanism, and presents stale numbers or past deadlines as current. A recently edited header does not refresh every fact inside it.\\r\\n- If a cache adds duplication without reliable upkeep, recommend bypassing it in retrieval and using the relevant index/source directly. Never automatically remove it. No cache is a valid architecture.\\r\\n- Stable context can live in a wiki; current metrics and task status should resolve to the appropriate live system or dated export. Exact terms should resolve to original records. Check source dates and flag conflicts rather than treating the wiki as automatically authoritative for every type of fact.\\r\\n- Check whether a fresh session could understand the user, resume the selected project, and find evidence without depending on this conversation. Praise compact, accurate routing; do not reward manual length, exhaustive root catalogs, or reading every wiki page.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Verify the other three Cs\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst run the required [routing and cross-runtime compatibility checks](compatibility.md). This covers stale/broken paths, important unlinked work, differences between operating manuals, and skill availability across the runtimes in use or targeted for migration. Compare shared behavior and required resources, not raw file equality alone. Record expected runtime differences separately from defects. The protocol also defines the mandatory compatibility table, evidence labels, and calibration cases.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Connections:** Identify applicable domains: finances, customer interactions, calendar, communication, tasks, meetings, and knowledge/files; add content or specialist domains when material. Record each domain\'s relevance, mechanism, specific access route, successful-read evidence, date, and limitations. One tool may cover multiple domains only when each has evidence. A configured MCP or `.env` key does not establish authentication. Use narrow, safe read-only checks when available, or dated successful-run evidence within the source\'s expected refresh interval. With no documented interval, use 30 days and explain why highly volatile data may require a fresh read. Do not print secret values or bulk private records. Do not run unknown scripts before checking their side effects.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Capabilities:** Select up to three workflows tied to the identified priorities, not merely the most polished demos. Examine triggers, inputs, output destination, examples of actual usable outputs, verification, failure handling, and repeated use. Missing workflows remain gaps. Check discoverability, needed supporting files, and canonical/mirror consistency after documented transforms. Agents are optional; quantity, complexity, and custom naming earn no bonus. Use existing outputs or safe bounded checks; do not start paid generation or substantive work just to audit it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cadence:** Inspect actual enabled schedules, event triggers, hooks, or explicitly defined human-run rituals and their execution records. Identify the host/runtime, trigger, expected output, last due execution, success/failure evidence, and stop/recovery controls. A skill named `daily-*`, a template, or recently edited files does not establish cadence. A useful manual ritual gets limited credit; label it manual. Do not call a local job unattended or laptop-independent without evidence for that environment. Judge monthly or quarterly routines against their real due dates rather than a fixed weekly window.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Score and prioritize\\r\\n\\r\\nRead the complete [rubric.md](rubric.md). Assign all 20 criterion scores, then apply its caps. Show the four subtotals, raw sum, any cap and reason, final score, and stage. Missing evidence earns no verified credit, but distinguish **unverified** from **known broken**. Record excluded domains with reasons; do not exclude a domain merely because it is disconnected.\\r\\n\\r\\nRank gaps by likely effect on the user\'s work, with wrong/stale answers and missing source access ahead of cosmetic tidiness. Give up to three concrete fixes with an exact affected route/workflow, supporting evidence, and a clear completion check. Do not invent three faults in a healthy system. Distinguish a repair from a check needed to resolve uncertainty.\\r\\n\\r\\nGroup findings into **confirmed defects**, **verification gaps**, and **improvement opportunities**; list intentional differences separately. Keep severity distinct from confidence. Assign each underlying issue a stable ID and reuse it across sections. Do not count one missing mirror or bad route as multiple independent defects. State which existing rubric criteria it affects; do not introduce a migration score or change the v2 anchors. Explain score limitations, including manual-cadence limits when relevant. Unchecked evidence is not proof of failure, and optional enhancements are not defects merely because they are absent.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Return a concise, reviewable report\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this structure, keeping the evidence ledger compact:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **AIOS Audit: date, project, rubric v2.** Scope, runtimes, verification limits, and a plain-language conclusion. Lead with what works and the most consequential mismatch. Report unique finding counts by class, not as an exhaustive total outside the inspected scope.\\r\\n2. **AGENTS.md / AGENTS.md findings:** always include the dedicated section specified below, even when no issues are found. Do not bury operating-manual findings in the general fixes or routing table.\\r\\n3. **Routing and migration compatibility:** the coverage/matrix and actionable findings from [compatibility.md](compatibility.md), including confirmed defects, verification gaps, intentional differences, and improvement opportunities. Reference existing finding IDs instead of repeating full manual findings. State whether sampled migration is blocked, needs verification, or passed the inspected checks; do not imply the whole system is portable from a sample.\\r\\n4. **Routing check:** five probe results, including source references and direct/fallback/unresolved status.\\r\\n5. **What works:** up to three evidence-backed strengths.\\r\\n6. **Verified operational reliability:** four rows, each `/25`; raw total, caps, final `/100`, and stage. Include criterion IDs and awarded points so arithmetic is reproducible. Explain separately how confirmed defects and unverified evidence limited credit. This is not an overall usefulness grade.\\r\\n7. **Top improvements:** up to three ranked actions, each labeled repair, verify, or optional improvement, with evidence, exact next action, expected practical benefit, and what would prove it complete. Refer to finding IDs instead of repeating them in full.\\r\\n8. **Progress since the previous audit:** link the baseline, show finding transitions and evidence from [history.md](history.md), and explain any comparable score change. Separate actual repairs, newly verified evidence, regressions, and coverage/rubric changes. No prior comparable report means a new baseline, not an invented improvement.\\r\\n9. **Next run:** a ready-to-use `/level-up` prompt carrying the highest-value gap, evidence, and acceptance check; use `/link <target> <purpose>` for a routing-only fix. Recommend these commands only if installed; otherwise give the equivalent plain-language task. `/grill-me`, if available, is useful for genuinely missing context, not for facts already stored elsewhere.\\r\\n10. **Saved record:** write and read back the complete report using [templates/report.md](templates/report.md). Link the actual saved file in the final reply. The chat can be concise; the saved record must retain the score breakdown, evidence, compatibility checks, and carried finding ledger. Never say saved without confirming it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Required section: AGENTS.md / AGENTS.md findings\\r\\n\\r\\nStart by naming the exact root manuals inspected and the scoped manuals sampled. Mark each root manual **checked**, **missing**, or **not checked**, with a reason where needed. State whether their shared guidance agrees, conflicts, or intentionally differs. Do not imply that inspecting one file verifies the other; a setup with only one applicable manual does not need a duplicate merely to pass.\\r\\n\\r\\nThen show a compact findings table:\\r\\n\\r\\n| File and section/line | Rule or missing route | Finding and practical effect | Recommended change |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Exact clickable file reference | Short quote or precise paraphrase; label omissions explicitly | What was verified, stale, conflicting, broken, or unverified, and how that affects finding or using information | Specific edit or verification step |\\r\\n\\r\\nSeparate **problems in the manual itself** from **problems in files it routes to**. For example, a stale target index is a downstream maintenance gap unless the manual also directs readers to the wrong index. Name the downstream file and the route that reaches it. Include one brief statement about what the manuals already do well. If no issues are found, explicitly say \\"No operating-manual issues found in the inspected scope\\" and state any coverage limits; do not invent findings to fill the table.\\r\\n\\r\\nFinish the section with whether any manuals were changed. In a standard read-only audit, say **\\"No operating manuals were changed.\\"** These findings inform the existing Four-Cs criteria and caps; do not add a fifth score or double-count deductions. Apply this section to both chat reports and saved reports.\\r\\n\\r\\nRecommend rerunning `/audit` after the selected fix and weekly during active setup, then at a sensible maintenance interval. Compare with a previous saved report only when scope and rubric match; v1 scores need a new baseline. Scores can decrease when evidence becomes stale. Do not promise a higher score from another run alone.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe audit is read-only toward inspected systems: no repairs, file moves, installation, scheduler changes, messages, or external writes. Its sole default write is a new local audit report under `audits/` (create the directory if needed). Preserve earlier reports, redact secrets and unnecessary private records, and do not update business memory or source-of-truth files from audit observations. On an incomplete run, save a clearly marked partial report when possible; do not present it as a completed audit or fabricate a score. If saving fails, report that explicitly and return the report in chat.\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: skill-installer\\r\\ndescription: Install Codex skills into $CODEX_HOME/skills from a curated list or a GitHub repo path. Use when a user asks to list installable skills, install a curated skill, or install a skill from another repo (including private repos).\\r\\nmetadata:\\r\\n  short-description: Install curated skills from openai/skills or other repos\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Skill Installer\\r\\n\\r\\nHelps install skills. By default these are from https://github.com/openai/skills/tree/main/skills/.curated, but users can also provide other locations. Experimental skills live in https://github.com/openai/skills/tree/main/skills/.experimental and can be installed the same way.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse the helper scripts based on the task:\\r\\n- List skills when the user asks what is available, or if the user uses this skill without specifying what to do. Default listing is `.curated`, but you can pass `--path skills/.experimental` when they ask about experimental skills.\\r\\n- Install from the curated list when the user provides a skill name.\\r\\n- Install from another repo when the user provides a GitHub repo/path (including private repos).\\r\\n\\r\\nInstall skills with the helper scripts.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Communication\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen listing skills, output approximately as follows, depending on the context of the user\'s request. If they ask about experimental skills, list from `.experimental` instead of `.curated` and label the source accordingly:\\r\\n\\"\\"\\"\\r\\nSkills from {repo}:\\r\\n1. skill-1\\r\\n2. skill-2 (already installed)\\r\\n3. ...\\r\\nWhich ones would you like installed?\\r\\n\\"\\"\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter installing a skill, tell the user it will be available on their next turn.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Scripts\\r\\n\\r\\nAll of these scripts use network, so when running in the sandbox, request escalation when running them.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `scripts/list-skills.py` (prints skills list with installed annotations)\\r\\n- `scripts/list-skills.py --format json`\\r\\n- Example (experimental list): `scripts/list-skills.py --path skills/.experimental`\\r\\n- `scripts/install-skill-from-github.py --repo <owner>/<repo> --path <path/to/skill> [<path/to/skill> ...]`\\r\\n- `scripts/install-skill-from-github.py --url https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/tree/<ref>/<path>`\\r\\n- Example (experimental skill): `scripts/install-skill-from-github.py --repo openai/skills --path skills/.experimental/<skill-name>`\\r\\n\\r\\n## Behavior and Options\\r\\n\\r\\n- Defaults to direct download for public GitHub repos.\\r\\n- If download fails with auth/permission errors, falls back to git sparse checkout.\\r\\n- Aborts if the destination skill directory already exists.\\r\\n- Installs into `$CODEX_HOME/skills/<skill-name>` (defaults to `~/.codex/skills`).\\r\\n- Multiple `--path` values install multiple skills in one run, each named from the path basename unless `--name` is supplied.\\r\\n- Options: `--ref <ref>` (default `main`), `--dest <path>`, `--method auto|download|git`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Notes\\r\\n\\r\\n- Curated listing is fetched from `https://github.com/openai/skills/tree/main/skills/.curated` via the GitHub API. If it is unavailable, explain the error and exit.\\r\\n- Private GitHub repos can be accessed via existing git credentials or optional `GITHUB_TOKEN`/`GH_TOKEN` for download.\\r\\n- Git fallback tries HTTPS first, then SSH.\\r\\n- The skills at https://github.com/openai/skills/tree/main/skills/.system are preinstalled, so no need to help users install those. If they ask, just explain this. If they insist, you can download and overwrite.\\r\\n- Installed annotations come from `$CODEX_HOME/skills`.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"92ad58","wall_time_seconds":2.7179533,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1113,"output":"124:- **image-to-code** — Elite website image-to-code skill for Codex. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"1fffa8","wall_time_seconds":1.5837142,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2542,"output":"---\\r\\nname: skill-doctor\\r\\ndescription: Use when the user wants their agent setup graded from real conversation history, asks which installed skills are actually working, or wants evidence-backed skill edits — scores recent local Claude Code / Codex sessions against efficiency and code-quality rubrics, then drafts skill changes gated by a deterministic aggregator and renders one local shareable report.\\r\\nargument-hint: \\"[optional: --repo PATH, --days N, or a question about the setup]\\"\\r\\nlicense: MIT\\r\\nmetadata:\\r\\n  version: 1.0.0\\r\\n  build_pattern: \\"Rebuild of warpdotdev/common-skills skill-doctor (MIT): harvest → LLM-judges-with-rubrics → deterministic aggregation gate → self-contained HTML report\\"\\r\\n  distinct_from: \\"skillopt-sleep (nightly automated replay loop with adopt gate; this is one interactive graded pass); write-a-skill (authors a skill from expertise; this improves skills from observed session evidence); self-eval (grades this session\'s own work; this grades a window of past sessions)\\"\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# skill-doctor — grade the agent setup from real sessions\\r\\n\\r\\n> **Privacy is the contract.** Everything runs locally. Transcripts are condensed,\\r\\n> secret-redacted, chmod-0600, and never uploaded — the only shareable artifact is\\r\\n> the report the user chooses to share.\\r\\n\\r\\nRun from the repo being graded. Every artifact goes to one fresh scratch dir, never\\r\\ninto the repo:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nRUN=\\"$(mktemp -d \\"${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/skill-doctor-XXXXXXXX\\")\\"\\r\\npython scripts/collect_sessions.py --out \\"$RUN\\"          # 1 — harvest + redact\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**1 — Collect.** Scans Claude Code project-history JSONL and Codex rollouts,\\r\\ndiscovers repo skills (`.claude/skills`, `.agents/skills`, `.codex/skills`, plugin\\r\\nlayouts), detects skill usage (`Skill` invocations, slash commands, SKILL.md paths), samples\\r\\nnewest-first, and writes redacted transcripts. Read `$RUN/inventory.json`: if\\r\\n`sessions_sampled` is 0, tell the user there is nothing recent to score (suggest\\r\\n`--days 90` or `--repo`) and stop. `skills_found` 0 is fine — the report becomes a\\r\\ncase for creating skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n**2 — Score.** `python scripts/score_aggregator.py --inventory \\"$RUN/inventory.json\\"\\r\\n--emit-template > \\"$RUN/session_scores.json\\"`. Read each transcript in\\r\\n`$RUN/transcripts/` and judge it against **both** rubrics — `scorers/efficiency.md`\\r\\nand `scorers/code-quality.md`. Fill the template with a **label from the rubric\'s\\r\\ntable** and a 1–3 sentence reason citing transcript specifics. Never invent numeric\\r\\nscores — the aggregator derives them from labels. Use `insufficient_evidence` when\\r\\na transcript shows no judgeable diff. Also write 1–5 `top_findings`: the most\\r\\nimpactful cross-session patterns, concrete and specific.\\r\\n\\r\\n**3 — Draft edits.** Follow `references/skill_edit_governance.md` (the filing bar:\\r\\nwould a competent agent with the current instructions still fail this way?). For\\r\\neach suggestion that clears it, write the full improved SKILL.md to\\r\\n`$RUN/proposed/<skill>/SKILL.md`, produce `diff -u <current> <proposed>`, and record\\r\\nit in `$RUN/suggestions.json` citing the sampled session id(s) that motivated it.\\r\\nZero suggestions is a valid success — say why per finding. Never modify the user\'s\\r\\nreal skill files in this step.\\r\\n\\r\\n**4 — Aggregate (the gate).** `python scripts/score_aggregator.py --inventory\\r\\n\\"$RUN/inventory.json\\" --scores \\"$RUN/session_scores.json\\" --suggestions\\r\\n\\"$RUN/suggestions.json\\"`. It validates labels against the rubric tables, refuses\\r\\nscores for unsampled sessions, requires substantive reasons, rejects suggestions\\r\\nthat cite no scored session, computes `overall = 0.5·efficiency +\\r\\n0.35·code_quality + 0.15·skill_coverage`, and writes `report.json`. **Exit 4 is a\\r\\nstop**: fix what it names and re-run; never hand-edit report.json around it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**5 — Render + tell.** `python scripts/render_report.py --report \\"$RUN/report.json\\"`\\r\\n→ one self-contained `report.html` (no JS, no CDN, dark-mode + print-to-PDF). Then\\r\\ntell the user the grade and the top findings in text, link\\r\\n`file://$RUN/report.html`, and ask whether to apply the proposed diffs to their\\r\\nreal skills — apply only on an explicit yes, skill by skill.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Hard rules\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Never upload transcripts, session files, or any excerpt.** Local only.\\r\\n2. **Labels only, from the rubric tables.** The aggregator owns all arithmetic.\\r\\n3. **Every suggestion traces to a scored session** — or it is dropped. Generic best practice is not evidence.\\r\\n4. **Zero suggestions is a success**, not a failure to report around.\\r\\n5. **Exit 4 from the aggregator is a stop**, not an error to swallow or bypass.\\r\\n6. **Never touch the user\'s real skill files** without an explicit per-skill yes; proposed edits live under `$RUN/proposed/`.\\r\\n7. **A proposed skill edit follows write-a-skill discipline** — trigger phrase in the description, smallest change that expresses the rule, replace over append.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Scripts\\r\\n\\r\\n| Script | Role | Exit codes |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| `scripts/collect_sessions.py` | Harvest Claude Code + Codex sessions, redact secrets, sample, inventory | 0 · 3 bad input |\\r\\n| `scripts/score_aggregator.py` | Validate labels/reasons/suggestions, compute grade, emit report.json | 0 · 2 warnings · 3 bad input · 4 validation failure |\\r\\n| `scripts/render_report.py` | report.json → single self-contained report.html | 0 · 3 bad input |\\r\\n\\r\\nAll support `--help`, `--output json`, and `--sample` (no real history needed).\\r\\n\\r\\n## References and assets\\r\\n\\r\\n- [`scorers/efficiency.md`](scorers/efficiency.md) · [`scorers/code-quality.md`](scorers/code-quality.md) — the two rubrics, preserved verbatim from upstream\\r\\n- [`references/transcript_scoring_canon.md`](references/transcript_scoring_canon.md) — why rubric-anchored LLM judging works and where it fails (7 sources)\\r\\n- [`references/session_mining_privacy.md`](references/session_mining_privacy.md) — the local-only contract, redaction pattern canon (7 sources)\\r\\n- [`references/skill_edit_governance.md`](references/skill_edit_governance.md) — the filing bar for proposing skill edits (7 sources)\\r\\n- [`assets/session_scores.example.json`](assets/session_scores.example.json) · [`assets/suggestions.example.json`](assets/suggestions.example.json) · [`assets/report.example.json`](assets/report.example.json) — the three handoff shapes\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: skill-lint\\r\\ndescription: Use when a repo skill fails, produces errors, gives wrong instructions, or references stale information. Also use when a command from a skill returns \\"not found\\" or \\"unknown flag\\", when a skill references a file path that doesn\'t exist, when a skill\'s behavior doesn\'t match the codebase, or to list available skills, manage local overrides, check staleness, or optimize token usage and quality.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Skill Doctor\\r\\n\\r\\nDiagnose issues with repository skills, manage local overrides, and optimize skill quality.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Scope:** Skills in the current repository\'s `.claude/skills/` directory only.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Routing\\r\\n\\r\\nParse `$ARGUMENTS`:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Pattern | Mode |\\r\\n|---------|------|\\r\\n| `list` | **list** (all repo skills) |\\r\\n| `info <skill>` | **list** (detail for one skill) |\\r\\n| `feedback [skill] \\"<message>\\"` | **feedback** (quick note) |\\r\\n| (empty) or `<skill-name>` | **diagnose** |\\r\\n| `--optimize <skill>` | **optimize** |\\r\\n| `--manage [skill]` | **manage** (status) |\\r\\n| `clone/diff/sync/revert <skill>` | **manage** (lifecycle) |\\r\\n| `report/gist/pr <skill>` | **deliver** |\\r\\n| `resolve <skill>` | **resolve** |\\r\\n\\r\\nUnrecognized input: show this table and ask to rephrase. Then **read `./reference.md`** for detailed procedures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## List\\r\\n\\r\\nDynamically discovers repo skills by reading `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`. Shows name, description, invocation, and override status. `info <skill>` reads the full SKILL.md to provide detailed usage guidance. All information comes from the skill files themselves — nothing is hardcoded. See reference.md for details.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Feedback\\r\\n\\r\\nLightweight path for quick notes. No investigation or quality gate — just scrub, preview, confirm, post. If skill name is omitted, ask which skill (or use the most recently invoked skill if known from conversation context). See reference.md for details.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Manage\\r\\n\\r\\nLifecycle: clone -> edit -> diff -> sync/revert -> pr/gist. Local overrides live at `~/.claude/skills/<name>/` with `.skill-origin.json` tracking.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Diagnose\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Identify** skill, validate it exists\\r\\n2. **Classify**: `invalid_command` | `wrong_assumption` | `stale_info` | `other`\\r\\n3. **Investigate** -- reproduce, root cause, determine fix, check related issues. Quality gate: confirmed problem + root cause + suggested fix\\r\\n4. **Duplicate check** in Discussion thread. Match found: upvote, add context, or report anyway\\r\\n5. **Route**: local override -> fix inline, stop. Repo -> offer clone-and-fix or report-only\\r\\n6. **Scrub** PII, **review** with developer, **deliver** to GitHub Discussion + Slack\\r\\n\\r\\n## Optimize\\r\\n\\r\\nChecks frontmatter, token efficiency (<500 words), structure quality, and staleness (file paths, commands, flags). Scored report with fixes. Local overrides fixed directly; repo versions cloned first.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Deliver & Resolve\\r\\n\\r\\n**Deliver**: GitHub Discussion (primary, one thread per skill) + Slack `#log-skill-feedback` (optional notification). Check prerequisites first (gh auth, repo access). See reference.md.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Resolve**: After fix PR merges, mark Discussion reports resolved, post Slack summary, offer to lock thread.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Common Mistakes\\r\\n\\r\\nSee reference.md for the full table. ## assistant

Closest installed fits:

- **`audit`**: best for duplicate identities, stale references, retired skills still active, and runtime compatibility. Read-only toward installed skills.
- **`skill-doctor`**: globally installed for Claude and Codex. Evaluates actual session history for efficiency and working skills, then proposes evidence-backed edits. Does not delete.
- **`skill-lint`**: globally installed for both, but explicitly scoped to repository `.claude/skills`. Checks stale commands, paths, token efficiency, and overrides. Its instructions are Claude-centric.
- **`skill-creator`** improves individual skills; **`skill-installer`** installs them. Neither provides collection cleanup.

No complete installed workflow found that inventories global/project/plugin skills, identifies waste and duplication, then removes an approved list while updating catalogs, parity, dependencies, and auto-update configuration. Use `audit` plus targeted `skill-doctor` evidence, with a separate explicitly authorized cleanup pass.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b04dbf","wall_time_seconds":2.6726493,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3238,"output":"---\\r\\nname: skill-lint\\r\\ndescription: Diagnose broken skill paths, missing dependencies, stale commands, conflicting runtime copies, or wasteful instructions; list skills and propose scoped local repairs.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Skill lint\\r\\n\\r\\nAudit or repair the skills the user names. Resolve the repository root and the loaded skill directory first. Codex project activations use `.agents/skills`; Claude uses `.claude/skills`. Also inspect `.codex/skills`, user-level runtime roots, and `skills/{codex,claude}` when they exist or the user asks for the catalog/global setup. Do not invent a `.Codex` directory or assume every runtime searches every root.\\r\\n\\r\\nRead [reference.md](reference.md) relative to this skill directory for procedures. This skill diagnoses package structure and instructions. `skill-doctor` is a separate optional history-based evaluation, not a prerequisite.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Modes\\r\\n\\r\\n- `list` or `info <skill>`: report actual names, descriptions, source locations, and required resources.\\r\\n- `<skill>` or an audit request: identify confirmed defects, verification gaps, and intentional runtime differences.\\r\\n- `--optimize <skill>`: improve trigger clarity, duplicated instructions, and useful detail. Length alone is not a defect.\\r\\n- `--manage`, `clone`, `diff`, `sync`, or `revert`: compare actual origins and copies; preserve local changes and explain the proposed operation.\\r\\n- `feedback`, `report`, `gist`, `pr`, or `resolve`: prepare a local draft. Publish or send only when the user explicitly authorizes that external action and identifies the destination.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Repair boundaries\\r\\n\\r\\nUse read-only checks first. Reproduce only with safe commands, help output, synthetic fixtures, or existing evidence. Do not run a substantive skill, paid generation, sends, or builds merely to lint it. Resolve supporting paths from the containing skill, commands from the documented working directory, and repository references from the repository root.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the user asks for repairs, apply confirmed scoped fixes and update the canonical/runtime copies that belong to that skill. Preserve supporting resources and declared differences. Do not replace independent local policies blindly, remove history, or delete packages without explicit authorization. Compare files before and after, verify links and metadata, and summarize what remains unverified.\\r\\n\\r\\nExternal reporting is optional. No default Discussion, Slack channel, repository, connector, or feedback service is assumed. Ordinary local diagnostics require no GitHub authentication or network calls.\\r\\n# Skill lint procedures\\r\\n\\r\\n## Discover and classify\\r\\n\\r\\nResolve the current project and actual runtime roots. Inspect immediate skill folders for `SKILL.md`, then read metadata before full instructions. The loaded package is an activation; a catalog is storage. Record both when a sharing policy names them. Missing `SKILL.md` does not authorize deleting a folder: it may contain assets, scripts, archives, or a project fork.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each suspected defect, record the exact source file, expected base directory, target or dependency, observed result, root cause, and proposed fix. Distinguish concrete paths from examples, placeholders, globs, remote-host paths, and optional resources. A tool unavailable in this session may be an alternative workflow or verification gap rather than a nonexistent dependency.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verify safely\\r\\n\\r\\nUse available file/search tools or `rg`, Python, and the host shell; do not assume Glob/Read/Bash tool names. On Windows use PowerShell-native path checks and commands. Check installed command help/version before relying on flags. Inspect unknown scripts for side effects before executing them. Use synthetic fixtures for scripts that otherwise consume history or perform external actions.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor duplicate copies, compare purpose, declared identity/aliases, frontmatter, supporting resources, and documented runtime adaptations. Preserve an independent implementation until its relationship is established. A transformed runtime copy need not be byte-identical to its source.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Manage local copies\\r\\n\\r\\nOrigin metadata such as `.skill-origin.json` is useful only when it actually exists; do not fabricate origins, commit counts, or precedence. Show a diff before rollback or destructive replacement. Follow the repository\'s catalog/activation policy. Record the exact changed copies and verify the resources they reference after synchronization. Do not modify unrelated runtime configuration or operating manuals as an incidental repair.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Feedback and delivery\\r\\n\\r\\nPrepare a concise local report or draft containing the defect, evidence, impact, and proposed remedy. Remove secrets and unnecessary private information. A request to diagnose, optimize, or fix a skill does not authorize a GitHub post, gist, PR, email, Slack message, upvote, resolution comment, or thread lock.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor an explicitly authorized external action, verify the named destination and available CLI/connector, show the concrete content when needed, and honor existing approval in the conversation without asking again. Report the actual result and link it. Do not assume a particular Discussion thread or channel exists.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Output\\r\\n\\r\\nReport inspected roots and skill count, confirmed fixes, preserved intentional differences, and unresolved verification gaps. Separate metadata/link checks from execution tests. State whether supporting files were preserved and whether any external action occurred.\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: skill-doctor\\r\\ndescription: Use when the user wants their agent setup graded from real conversation history, asks which installed skills are actually working, or wants evidence-backed skill edits — scores recent local Claude Code / Codex sessions against efficiency and code-quality rubrics, then drafts skill changes gated by a deterministic aggregator and renders one local shareable report.\\r\\nlicense: MIT\\r\\nmetadata:\\r\\n  argument_hint: \\"[optional: --repo PATH, --days N, or a question about the setup]\\"\\r\\n  version: 1.0.0\\r\\n  build_pattern: \\"Rebuild of warpdotdev/common-skills skill-doctor (MIT): harvest → LLM-judges-with-rubrics → deterministic aggregation gate → self-contained HTML report\\"\\r\\n  distinct_from: \\"skillopt-sleep (nightly automated replay loop with adopt gate; this is one interactive graded pass); write-a-skill (authors a skill from expertise; this improves skills from observed session evidence); self-eval (grades this session\'s own work; this grades a window of past sessions)\\"\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# skill-doctor — grade the agent setup from real sessions\\r\\n\\r\\nThe bundled scripts parse and write files locally and make no model calls. Scoring by this assistant sends the excerpts it reads to the configured model provider as part of the conversation. A fully local assessment requires a locally hosted model. Redaction is best-effort; inspect only the minimum needed excerpts and never claim provider-side processing is local-only.\\r\\n\\r\\nResolve all script, scorer, asset, and reference paths from **this loaded skill directory**, not the repository being graded. Resolve the target repository separately and pass it as `--repo`. Use a fresh directory under the host\'s temporary directory for outputs. On Windows use PowerShell or Python temporary-directory creation; on POSIX `mktemp -d` is an alternative.\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\npython \\"<skill-root>/scripts/collect_sessions.py\\" --repo \\"<repo-root>\\" --out \\"<run-dir>\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace placeholders with resolved absolute paths before execution. The Python scripts attempt POSIX 0700/0600 permissions; those modes do not establish restrictive Windows ACLs. Do not claim Windows permissions were secured without checking them. Do not collect actual history during installation or instruction repair; use `--help` or `--sample` for checks.\\r\\n\\r\\n**1 — Collect.** Scans Claude Code project-history JSONL and Codex rollouts,\\r\\ndiscovers repo skills (`.claude/skills`, `.agents/skills`, `.codex/skills`, plugin\\r\\nlayouts), detects skill usage (`Skill` invocations, slash commands, SKILL.md paths), samples\\r\\nnewest-first, and writes redacted transcripts. Read `<run-dir>/inventory.json`: if\\r\\n`sessions_sampled` is 0, tell the user there is nothing recent to score (suggest\\r\\n`--days 90` or `--repo`) and stop. `skills_found` 0 is fine — the report becomes a\\r\\ncase for creating skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n**2 — Score.** `python \\"<skill-root>/scripts/score_aggregator.py\\" --inventory \\"<run-dir>/inventory.json\\"\\r\\n--emit-template > \\"<run-dir>/session_scores.json\\"`. Read each transcript in\\r\\n`<run-dir>/transcripts/` and judge it against **both** rubrics — `scorers/efficiency.md`\\r\\nand `scorers/code-quality.md`. Fill the template with a **label from the rubric\'s\\r\\ntable** and a 1–3 sentence reason citing transcript specifics. Never invent numeric\\r\\nscores — the aggregator derives them from labels. Use `insufficient_evidence` when\\r\\na transcript shows no judgeable diff. Also write 1–5 `top_findings`: the most\\r\\nimpactful cross-session patterns, concrete and specific.\\r\\n\\r\\n**3 — Draft edits.** Follow `references/skill_edit_governance.md` (the filing bar:\\r\\nwould a competent agent with the current instructions still fail this way?). For\\r\\neach suggestion that clears it, write the full improved SKILL.md to\\r\\n`<run-dir>/proposed/<skill>/SKILL.md`, produce a unified diff using Python `difflib` or an available diff tool, and record\\r\\nit in `<run-dir>/suggestions.json` citing the sampled session id(s) that motivated it.\\r\\nZero suggestions is a valid success — say why per finding. Never modify the user\'s\\r\\nreal skill files in this step.\\r\\n\\r\\n**4 — Aggregate (the gate).** `python \\"<skill-root>/scripts/score_aggregator.py\\" --inventory\\r\\n\\"<run-dir>/inventory.json\\" --scores \\"<run-dir>/session_scores.json\\" --suggestions\\r\\n\\"<run-dir>/suggestions.json\\"`. It validates labels against the rubric tables, refuses\\r\\nscores for unsampled sessions, requires substantive reasons, rejects suggestions\\r\\nthat cite no scored session, computes `overall = 0.5·efficiency +\\r\\n0.35·code_quality + 0.15·skill_coverage`, and writes `report.json`. **Exit 4 is a\\r\\nstop**: fix what it names and re-run; never hand-edit report.json around it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**5 — Render + tell.** `python \\"<skill-root>/scripts/render_report.py\\" --report \\"<run-dir>/report.json\\"`\\r\\n→ one self-contained `report.html` (no JS, no CDN, dark-mode + print-to-PDF). Then\\r\\ntell the user the grade and the top findings in text, link\\r\\nthe actual absolute local path to `report.html` in a Markdown link, and apply the proposed diffs only if already authorized; otherwise ask whether to apply them — apply only within the scope of explicit user authorization.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Hard rules\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **No separate uploads or publication of transcripts.** Minimize excerpts processed by the configured assistant; disclose provider processing and use a local model if the user requires fully local scoring.\\r\\n2. **Labels only, from the rubric tables.** The aggregator owns all arithmetic.\\r\\n3. **Every suggestion traces to a scored session** — or it is dropped. Generic best practice is not evidence.\\r\\n4. **Zero suggestions is a success**, not a failure to report around.\\r\\n5. **Exit 4 from the aggregator is a stop**, not an error to swallow or bypass.\\r\\n6. **Never touch the user\'s real skill files** without user authorization covering those edits; proposed edits live under `<run-dir>/proposed/`.\\r\\n7. **A proposed skill edit uses concise skill-authoring discipline** — trigger phrase in the description, smallest change that expresses the rule, replace over append.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Scripts\\r\\n\\r\\n| Script | Role | Exit codes |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| `scripts/collect_sessions.py` | Harvest Claude Code + Codex sessions, redact secrets, sample, inventory | 0 · 3 bad input |\\r\\n| `scripts/score_aggregator.py` | Validate labels/reasons/suggestions, compute grade, emit report.json | 0 · 2 warnings · 3 bad input · 4 validation failure |\\r\\n| `scripts/render_report.py` | report.json → single self-contained report.html | 0 · 3 bad input |\\r\\n\\r\\nAll support `--help`, `--output json`, and `--sample` (no real history needed).\\r\\n\\r\\n## References and assets\\r\\n\\r\\n- [`scorers/efficiency.md`](scorers/efficiency.md) · [`scorers/code-quality.md`](scorers/code-quality.md) — the two rubrics, preserved verbatim from upstream\\r\\n- [`references/transcript_scoring_canon.md`](references/transcript_scoring_canon.md) — why rubric-anchored LLM judging works and where it fails (7 sources)\\r\\n- [`references/session_mining_privacy.md`](references/session_mining_privacy.md) — local-script handling, provider-processing limits, and redaction patterns (7 sources)\\r\\n- [`references/skill_edit_governance.md`](references/skill_edit_governance.md) — the filing bar for proposing skill edits (7 sources)\\r\\n- [`assets/session_scores.example.json`](assets/session_scores.example.json) · [`assets/suggestions.example.json`](assets/suggestions.example.json) · [`assets/report.example.json`](assets/report.example.json) — the three handoff shapes\\r\\n"}'}]

## assistant

No remaining material bug found in the reviewed changes.

The new effective-pair XOR check catches a missing runtime activation while allowing both counterparts to remain inactive. Link normalization requires matching existing resolved targets and identical anchors. Index support-folder classification and copy wording are consistent.

`skill-smoke.py` is limited to metadata, entry-point existence, and working-directory checks; clarify its PASS wording as planned. Reviewed lint and doctor repairs preserve scope and remove unsupported tooling/privacy claims.

No files changed or mutating helpers executed.
